Title: Collectanea de Cyrillo Lucario, Patriarcha Constantinopolitano, quorum syllabum aversa pagina exhibet; quibus accessere De veteris Graecae Ecclesiae hymnis et Theologica de causis remediisque dissidiorum (a collection of documents on Cyril Lucaris, with a narrative of his life and two further tracts)
Creator: Thomas Smith (1638-1710), of Magdalen College, Oxford, formerly chaplain to the English embassy at Constantinople (collector, narrator and editor); the appended documents include letters of Cyril Lucaris and Ottoman and Western papers in Latin and Italian
Publisher: London (imprint illegible in the OCR; the host catalogue gives 1707)
Publication Date: 1707
Rights: Public Domain (printed 1707)
Language: lat
Source: Internet Archive item bim_eighteenth-century_collectanea-de-cyrillo-l_smith-thomas_1707, file bim_eighteenth-century_collectanea-de-cyrillo-l_smith-thomas_1707_djvu.txt (eighteenth-century microfilm scan; Internet Archive contributor), fetched directly by the library/source-research thread (Era VII session, one-world agent for Orthodoxy under the Ottomans), 2026-10-16; host rights statement: none given in the item record; the rights basis is the 1707 print date
Provenance note: Header prepended; the OCR text follows unchanged apart from long s normalised to s (8955 occurrences). Rights basis restated: a book printed in London in 1707 is public domain by date.
Content note: SECOND WITNESS: the roman-type Latin is readable with ordinary long-s and ligature errors; the engraved title page and the contents list on its verso are OCR noise, so the contents are described from the running heads. Order in the file: title and preface (about lines 400-1350, with the heading 'Narratio Historica turbarum' at 565); 'Narratio de vita, studiis, gestis et martyrio Cyrilli Lucarii' under that running head from about line 1357 to 4395; an Appendix of documents from about line 4395 (Lucaris's letters and papers in Latin; an Italian 'Instruttione' at about line 5786 and a 'Comandamento', an Ottoman command in Italian, at about line 6787, with 'Turcarum' at 6822); 'De veteris Graecae Ecclesiae hymnis' from about line 6994; the theological tract on the causes and remedies of dissensions from about line 8637. Mostly Latin with some Italian; the OCR holds almost no Greek characters, so any Greek in the original is lost here. An outside Anglican editor's frame; the embedded letters and the Ottoman papers are the primary matter.

Quare nihil tibi præstare non maluerim, quam

plurimorum mentes adeo occupasse, ut novum

rem, propriæ magis satisfactionis, quam alter-

tum apud me possis: etsi prudentia quidem re- 
pugnet, & alio me avocet negotiorum meorum 
ratio. Rem ipsam, ut cognovi, habeto.

non parum promoverant commoda; nec non ex
